264	### Versioning
265	
266	Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based
267	on scope:
268	
269	- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump
270	  directly to `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
271	- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-0`,
272	  implement across multiple commits incrementing the numeric
273	  suffix. The final commit drops the suffix.
274	
275	The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before
276	starting.
277	
278	A plan that picks up a `## Todo` item — or *re-scopes* an
279	existing `## In Progress` ladder to absorb one — **moves that
280	entry into `## In Progress` in the same commit** (the
281	version-bump / plan commit):
282	
283	- **Pickup:** the entry's text moves out of `## Todo` and into
284	  `## In Progress` — its overview and to-do list, followed by
285	  the plan ladder — so the section is self-describing, not a
286	  bare ladder. The chores opener **mirrors** that content so
287	  the cycle stays readable once `## In Progress` is cleared.
288	- **Close-out:** the item is removed from `## In Progress` and
289	  a one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) is added to
290	  `## Done`.
291	
292	`## In Progress` is the sole live record of the work between
293	those points. A cycle that merges several entangled `## Todo`
294	items moves all of them. A `## Todo` entry that duplicates
295	current `## In Progress` work is a process bug.
296	
297	For a single-step or squash close-out cycle, pickup and
298	close-out both land before the one commit, so the populated
299	`## In Progress` is a working-copy/review artifact that never
300	reaches committed history — expected; the chores opener is
301	then the durable capture.
302	
303	For multi-step:
304	
305	1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-0` with the plan and commit as a chore
306	   marker.
307	2. Implement in one or more `X.Y.Z-N` commits (increment N as
308	   needed).
309	3. Final commit bumps to `X.Y.Z` (no suffix), updates
310	   `notes/todo.md` and `notes/chores/chores-*.md` — this is the "done"
311	   marker.
312	
313	Multi-step cycles place the plan ladder in
314	`notes/todo.md > ## In Progress`, below the picked-up item's
315	text, as a bullet list with `(done)` / `(current)` markers —
316	see the file's intro paragraph for the format. When starting a new step, the *first* edit is to mark
317	that step `(current)` in `notes/todo.md` — before any code/doc
318	work — so the In Progress view reflects what's actually
319	happening. The flip back to `(done)` is part of the pre-commit
320	checklist (item 6).
321	
322	**Why numeric suffixes (`-0`, `-1`, …) rather than `-devN`:**
323	semver pre-release identifiers may consist of a single numeric
324	component, and they compare numerically per spec. So
325	`X.Y.Z-1 < X.Y.Z-2 < … < X.Y.Z` correctly orders the dev ladder
326	below the done marker. Cargo accepts this form. The `-dev` prefix
327	adds no information the git log doesn't already convey and
328	doubles typing per commit.
329	
330	The final release commit (no suffix) signals completion rather than
331	amending prior commits. This keeps history readable and makes it easy
332	to see which commits were exploratory vs final.
333	
334	For decomposition finer than `X.Y.Z-N` — sub-steps (`X.Y.Z-N.M`)
335	or sub-sub-steps (`X.Y.Z-N.M-K`) — see
336	[Sub-step Workflow](#sub-step-workflow). The cycle close-out
337	choice (squash to one commit vs land each sub-step separately)
338	is made at close-out, not cycle start.
339	
340	See also [`notes/substep-protocol.md`](notes/substep-protocol.md)
341	for the full protocol — when to use, per-substep contract
342	(`cargo test --bins` is non-negotiable), navigation, the validated
343	close-out squash recipe, and recovery. Revset primitives the
344	protocol relies on (`@`, `@-`, `..`, `::`, prefix matching) live in
345	[`notes/jj-revsets.md`](notes/jj-revsets.md).
346	
347	### Headings and entries that record a commit
348	
349	A `chores-NN.md` `##` section header that records a specific
350	commit, the matching `todo.md > ## Done` entry, and any `[N]`
351	reference to that section all use **exactly that commit's
352	title** — `<type>: <desc> (<version>)`, the same string the
353	commit gets (see [Commit Message Style](#commit-message-style)).
354	E.g. the chores header `## refactor: port push to Context
355	(0.48.0-6)` and the Done line `- refactor: port push to Context
356	(0.48.0-6) [[3]]`. For a multi-step cycle the `## Done` entry
357	uses the close-out commit's title.
358	
359	This does **not** apply to organizational headings (`## Todo`,
360	`## In Progress`, `# References`) or to design `###` subsections
361	inside a chores section — those are named for whatever fits.
362	Among the commit-recording ones, exact match is the strong
363	default (nothing absolute): a near-miss just makes it harder to
364	line a record up with its commit.
365	
366	A commit-recording header is provisional while the work is in
367	progress; the *last* edit before `vc-x1 push` syncs it — and the
368	`## Done` entry / `[N]` anchor for that commit — to the final
369	commit title. See [Markdown anchor links](#markdown-anchor-links)
370	for the slug algorithm; the pre-commit checklist catches a
371	dangling `#anchor`, and a future `vc-x1 validate-repo` should too
372	(and should verify the recorded title matches the commit).
373	
374	Existing sections and `## Done` entries (most of chores-01..09;
375	the pre-`0.48.1` `## Done` lines) predate this and keep their
376	free-form text; the convention applies going forward — `0.48.2`
377	converts the `0.48.1` section + its Done entry as the worked
378	example.
379	
380	### Chores section content — no edit list; git is the record
381	
382	A chores section is: a `Commits:` line (first line under the
383	header — see below), then [Prose form](#prose-form) (intro +
384	bullets) for what landed and why, and any `###` design
385	subsections. Bullets here are **conceptual** — design points,
386	structural notes — never a per-file edit list. That lives in the
387	commit message body, which is the source of truth for "what
388	changed mechanically" (immutable, `git show`-able, naturally
389	scoped to the commit). The chores section is the source of truth
390	for the design thinking; the two cross-link, neither restates the
391	other.
392	
393	When the intro starts wanting to explain a mechanism,
394	hypothesis, or wrinkle, don't inflate it — promote that to its
395	own `###` subsection inside the same `chores-NN.md`. If the
396	wrinkle is a live design concern (something that *should*
397	change, not just be recorded), also add a `notes/todo.md` item
398	with a `[N]` ref pointing at that subsection (todo→chores is the
399	normal ref direction).
400	
401	**Why:** a chores edit list and the commit body were specified
402	to be the same content in two places — and detail written twice
403	drifts. Git owns the mechanical record; chores owns the
404	narrative; `Commits:` links them.
405	
406	### Chores commit references
407	
408	The first line under a chores section header is a `Commits:`
409	line citing the git commit(s) that section records:
410	
411	```
412	## refactor: port push to Context (0.48.0-6)
413	
414	Commits: [[3]]
415	
416	<intro paragraph...>
417	```
418	
419	`Commits:` uses the file-local `[N]` reference machinery (see
420	[notes/README.md](notes/README.md#reference-numbering)),
421	**double-bracketed** so the brackets render — `Commits: [[3]]`,
422	or `Commits: [[3]],[[5]]` for several. (`[[3]]` shows as a
423	literal `[`, the `[3]` link, then a literal `]`; the inner
424	`[3]` resolves against its `[3]:` definition — CommonMark /
425	GitHub / VS Code all do this.) The `# References` definition
426	puts the **commit URL** as the destination, with the **full
427	40-hex SHA** in the title slot:
428	
429	```
430	[3]: https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/commit/<12-hex> "<40-hex>"
431	```
432	
433	- The 12-hex short SHA in the URL keeps it short; GitHub /
434	  GitLab resolve a unique prefix to the canonical commit page
435	  (GitLab's path has a `/-/` before `commit/`).
436	- The full SHA in the title is host-agnostic and unambiguous —
437	  it survives a repo host change, `git show <40-hex>` works in
438	  any clone, and external tooling scraping the notes (a
439	  database, say) gets the canonical identifier.
440	
441	**Timing.** The commit doesn't exist when its chores section is
442	written, so the `Commits:` line is **backfilled when the next
443	change to that repo is started** — the cycle-start step grabs
444	the just-pushed commit's URL + SHA and fills it in. The single
445	newest section is briefly `Commits:`-less; that's fine — the
446	commit itself is the record, and `git log --grep "(X.Y.Z)"`
447	finds it.
448	
449	### Pre-commit checklist
450	
451	At cycle *start* (before the version bump): (a) backfill the
452	previous chores section's `Commits:` ref with the just-pushed
453	commit's URL + full SHA — see
454	[Chores commit references](#chores-commit-references); (b) if
455	this cycle picks up a `## Todo` item (or re-scopes `## In
456	Progress` to absorb one), move that `## Todo` entry's text
457	into `## In Progress` and mirror it in the chores opener —
458	see [Versioning](#versioning).
459	
460	Before proposing a commit, run all of the following and fix any issues:
461	
462	1. `cargo fmt`
463	2. `cargo clippy`
464	3. `cargo test`
465	4. `cargo install --path . --locked` (if applicable) — `--locked`
466	   is required: without it, `cargo install` ignores `Cargo.lock`
467	   and re-resolves from scratch, which can pick incompatible
468	   versions even when `cargo build` / `cargo test` succeed.
469	5. Retest after install
470	6. Update `notes/todo.md` — for multi-step cycles, flip the
471	   just-completed step's marker from `(current)` to `(done)`
472	   **before** running `vc-x1 push`. The commit being pushed
473	   should reflect the new state. (The next step's `(current)`
474	   marker is set later, at the *start* of that step — see the
475	   Versioning multi-step section.)
476	7. Update `notes/todo.md` — at cycle close-out (final commit),
477	   remove the picked-up item from `## In Progress` and add a
478	   one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) to `## Done`.
479	8. Update `notes/chores/chores-*.md` — add a section (header =
480	   provisional commit title; intro paragraph + any `###` design
481	   subsections; **no** per-file edit list — that's the commit
482	   body).
483	9. Sync the chores section header to the **final** commit title
484	   and update every markdown anchor back-reference to it.
485	10. Update `notes/README.md` — if functionality changed (new
486	    flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).
487	
488	## Sub-step Workflow
489	
490	Larger work decomposes into named depths. The version-suffix
491	scheme nests:
492	
493	- **Single step** — one change, one commit + push + finalize.
```

Within the ladder it does *not* hold for nested\nclose-outs — `X.Y.Z-3` sorts *before* its children\n`X.Y.Z-3.0` / `X.Y.Z-3.1`, because a prefix always precedes\nits extensions. Cosmetic — nothing depends on within-ladder\norder — but don't claim monotonicity.\n\n**Why numeric suffixes** (`-0`, `-1`, …) rather than\n`-devN`: semver pre-release identifiers compare per spec and\nCargo accepts them. `-dev` adds no information the git log\ndoesn't carry and doubles typing per commit. The final\nclose-out commit (bare `X.Y.Z`) signals completion rather\nthan amending prior commits — history stays readable,\nexploratory vs final commits obvious.\n\n**push and squash are discretionary.** A cycle's commits\naccumulate locally; **push** and **squash** are not modes\nfixed at cycle start but actions taken when wanted:\n\n- **push** — to back up work or to publish interim\n  progress. 497	    flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).
498	
499	## Sub-step Workflow
500	
501	Larger work decomposes into named depths. The version-suffix
502	scheme nests:
503	
504	- **Single step** — one change, one commit + push + finalize.
505	- **Multi-step** — planned series within a target bump:
506	  `0.5.0 → 0.6.0-0`, `0.6.0-0 → 0.6.0-1`, …, `0.6.0-N → 0.6.0`.
507	- **Sub-step** — finer granularity within a step:
508	  `0.6.0-3.1 → 0.6.0-3.2 → … → 0.6.0-3` (close-out).
509	- **Sub-sub-step** — finer still:
510	  `0.6.0-3.4-0 → 0.6.0-3.4-1 → … → 0.6.0-3.4` (close-out).
511	
512	The conventions below apply at **whichever leaf depth the
513	current cycle planned to** — sub-step, sub-sub-step, etc.
514	"Sub-step" reads as shorthand for that leaf level throughout
515	this section.
516	
517	### Version suffix in titles and Cargo.toml
518	
519	Sub-step commits carry the leaf-level version in commit titles
520	**and** `Cargo.toml`. So `vc-x1 -V` shows the active sub-step
521	at build time. Bump Cargo.toml at the start of each sub-step
522	(extends the existing `X.Y.Z-N` step-start bump rule down a
523	level).
524	
525	Cargo accepts arbitrary numeric segments in semver pre-release
526	identifiers; lexical comparison gives the expected ordering
527	(`0.6.0-3.4-1 < 0.6.0-3.4-2 < 0.6.0-3.4`).
528	
529	### todo.md status flips
530	
531	Markers in `notes/todo.md > ## In Progress` flip on a defined
532	cadence:
533	
534	- **Start of (M):** mark (M) `(current)` as the first edit.
535	- **End of (M):** flip (M) `(current)` → `(done)` **before**
536	  the cargo cycle and commit. The commit captures the
537	  completed state.
538	- **Start of (M+1):** mark (M+1) `(current)` as that
539	  sub-step's first edit.
540	
541	Each sub-step's commit carries the "this sub-step is done"
542	record; the next sub-step's commit carries "next sub-step
543	starts."
544	
545	### Pre-commit cargo cycle (per sub-step)
546	
547	Run before every sub-step commit (not just at cycle close-out):
548	
549	1. `cargo fmt`
550	2. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`
551	3. `cargo test`
552	4. `cargo install --path . --locked`
553	5. (re-test if anything substantive)
554	
555	Keeps every intermediate commit buildable so bisection works
556	across the cycle's stack.
557	
558	### Commit-first review model
559	
560	Per sub-step:
561	
562	1. Make the sub-step changes.
563	2. Run the cargo cycle.
564	3. **Commit immediately** (both repos with ochid trailers, no
565	   separate approval gate at sub-step granularity).
566	4. Summarize the commit briefly in chat.
567	5. User reviews the committed revision in their editor (full
568	   file context, not chat-pasted diffs).
569	6. User iterates if needed; bot squashes follow-ups into the
570	   sub-step commit via `jj squash --into @-` (and
571	   `jj describe @-` — plus the chores section header and its
572	   anchor back-refs — if the title needs to change).
573	7. User signals "go to (M+1)" to advance.
574	
575	This **replaces** the per-sub-step review pause that the
576	top-level `### Review before proposing the commit block` and
577	`### Per-file review checkpoints` rules would otherwise
578	impose. Local jj commits are mutable until close-out, so
579	committing freely is safe.
580	
581	The two-gate ceremony (review + message approval) is preserved
582	for the **cycle-level push** at close-out — that crosses the
583	local→remote boundary and warrants explicit approval.
584	
585	### Ochid trailers on sub-step commits
586	
587	Sub-step commits include ochid trailers paired across the two
588	repos (same shape as top-level cycle commits):
589	
590	- App body: `ochid: /.claude/<.claude-chid>`
591	- `.claude` body: `ochid: /<app-chid>`
592	
593	Use `vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -L` to capture both pre-commit
594	change IDs (first line app, second `.claude`).
595	
596	### `.claude` cadence
597	
598	`.claude` commits **per sub-step alongside the app repo**.
599	Each sub-step's `.claude` commit captures the session state at
600	that moment.
601	
602	Alternative considered (`.claude` accumulates across the cycle
603	and commits once at close-out) was rejected — keeping the
604	per-sub-step pairing preserves flexibility (any sub-step can
605	be promoted to its own push without restructuring).
606	
607	**`.claude` is a linear journal — all session work lives on
608	`main`.** The repo has no need for parallel feature-branch
609	bookmarks to mirror app-side branches. When the app sits on
610	e.g. `init-clone-refactor`, `.claude` still commits to `main`.
611	Cross-references between sides are carried by the `ochid:`
612	trailer + commit timestamps; that's enough to associate
613	session activity with whichever app-side branch the cycle was
614	on.
615	
616	**Do not create or maintain `.claude` bookmarks that mirror
617	app-side branches.** This was tried once during 0.41.1-6.7 on
618	the impression that an app-side fork needed a `.claude`
619	partner; the partner bookmark went unused for the cycle and
620	the work landed on `.claude main` regardless. Keeping such
621	bookmarks risks the bot misreading them as "this branch needs
622	to advance" and steering session pushes to the wrong remote
623	ref.
624	
625	### Cycle close-out — squash or keep separate?
626	
627	Two valid shapes for landing the sub-step stack on `main`:
628	
629	- **Squash to one cycle commit** — single entry on `main`;
630	  sub-step granularity preserved only in the commit body's
631	  edit list. Right when the work is one logical change with
632	  intermediate validation points. **Cost:** the per-sub-step
633	  chores sections collapse into one, whose header becomes the
634	  single close-out commit title, and every anchor back-ref to a
635	  now-gone per-sub-step section gets re-pointed — real work,
636	  done as part of the squash.
637	- **Keep separate (N + 1 commits)** when the decomposition is
638	  itself informative (different conceptual stages, design
639	  progression worth showing in `git log`). Used on
640	  `0.41.1-6.7` (8 sub-sub-step commits + 1 close-out commit).
641	  Each section keeps its own header / `Commits:` ref — no
642	  consolidation churn.
643	
644	Pick at close-out, not at cycle start. No firm default — weigh
645	the squash's chores-consolidation cost against a cleaner
646	`git log`; that cost biases toward keeping separate unless the
647	decomposition genuinely isn't informative.
648	
649	### Reviewing committed sub-steps
650	
651	The commit-first model assumes the reviewer can read the diff
652	of an already-committed revision. Don't `jj edit -r @-` back
653	into a past commit to view it — that marks the commit mutable,
654	shifts the WC pointer, forces a `jj new -r <head>` recovery.
655	Use one of:
656	
657	**Terminal (always works):**
658	
659	```
660	jj diff -r @-                  # diff of previous commit
661	jj diff --from <X> --to <Y>    # diff between two arbitrary revs
662	jj show -r <X>                 # description + diff for one rev
663	jj log -r @-..@                # what's between two points
664	```
665	
666	Pipe through `delta` / `diff-so-fancy | less -R` for color and
667	side-by-side.
668	
669	**External diff tool** — configure jj to launch your editor:
670	
671	```
672	# ~/.config/jj/config.toml
673	[ui]
674	diff-editor = ["zed", "--diff", "$left", "$right"]
675	```
676	
677	Then `jj diff -r @-` opens the configured tool. Works for
678	arbitrary `--from`/`--to` ranges.
679	
680	**VS Code** — Source Control panel → Commit Graph →
681	right-click commit A → "Copy Commit ID" → right-click commit
682	B → "Compare with…" → paste / pick A. Two-commit diff opens
683	with the changed-files list. GitLens extension adds richer
684	"Open Comparison" actions; `Git: Compare with…` in the
685	Command Palette is a fallback.
686	
687	## Code Conventions
688	
689	### Doc comments on every file, function, and method
690	
691	Every `.rs` file must begin with a `//!` module docstring. Every
692	function and method must have a `///` doc comment. Keep them brief —
693	one sentence of purpose is often enough; the discipline is that the
694	comment exists, not that it be long. Doc comments follow the
695	[Prose form](#prose-form) shape (intro + bullets).
696	
697	This is a deliberate override of the generic "write no comments"
698	default that applies to inline `//` comments. Doc comments on the
699	module / item surface are expected; inline explanatory comments
700	inside function bodies remain discouraged unless they capture a
701	non-obvious WHY.
702	
703	**Clap-derive args:** doc comments on `#[arg(...)]` fields drive
704	`--help` output. Clap reflows by default and collapses bullets
705	into running prose. Add `#[arg(verbatim_doc_comment, ...)]` on
706	any field whose doc comment uses bullets so each `- …` lands on
```

```
185	not the exception; proposing commit text too early creates noise
186	and signals that I consider the work done when it usually isn't.
187	
188	Reviewing a unit of work typically takes a few loops before the
189	user and I agree it is right. The commit title and body are
190	drafted only *after* that agreement — they are a separate step,
191	never bundled into the same message as the work summary.
192	
193	This applies per-step in a multi-step flow too — each step gets a
194	review pause before its commit block appears.
195	
196	Signals that review is complete include explicit approval ("let's
197	commit", "looks good, commit it") **and any directive to start the
198	next step** ("do step 4", "next", "go N+1"). In that case the
199	previous step must be committed first — always commit the current
200	step before starting the next; don't ask.
201	
202	### Per-file review checkpoints
203	
204	The "review before commit block" rule applies to in-progress work
205	too, at finer grain. After each file edit, STOP, summarize what
206	changed, and wait for explicit go-ahead before touching the next
207	file or running follow-on commands (cargo test/install, dogfood
208	invocations, `vc-x1 …`, etc.).
209	
210	**Exceptions** — both ride along with the change they accompany,
211	no separate review pause:
212	
213	- **Refactors where code is moving across files** — e.g.
214	  lifting helpers into a new module and updating the original
215	  file's imports in the same change. Reviewing each half in
216	  isolation isn't useful.
217	- **`Cargo.toml` version bumps** that go with a specific code
218	  change.
219	
220	**Why:** the user reviews diffs as work lands. Chaining multiple
221	file edits without pausing forces them to untangle cumulative
222	state instead of inspecting each step in isolation.
223	
224	**How to apply:**
225	
226	- "continue", "go", "do it", "lg" approves *the next unit
227	  only*, not all remaining units of a multi-file plan. A green
228	  light to proceed with a *direction* is not a green light to
229	  skip per-step checkpoints.
230	- After landing a unit: brief summary (2–5 lines) and stop. No
231	  cargo build / test / install / dogfood runs unless the user
232	  explicitly asks.
233	- In addition to (not replacement for) the
234	  Review-before-commit-block rule above.
235	- **Sub-step exception:** within a multi-step cycle's
236	  sub-step (or sub-sub-step) ladder, the per-step checkpoints
237	  defer to the commit-first review model — see
238	  [Sub-step Workflow > Commit-first review model](#commit-first-review-model).
239	  Sub-step commits land as the bot finishes them; user reviews
240	  the committed revision in their editor. The cycle-level
241	  push at close-out keeps the two-gate ceremony.
242	
243	### Notes references
244	
245	Reference *citations* are double-bracketed so the brackets render
246	— `[[N]]`, or `[[2]],[[3]]` for several (comma-separated, not
247	`[2,3]` or `[[2]][[3]]`). The `[N]:` definitions in a file's
248	`# References` section and inline `[text](url)` / `[text](#anchor)`
249	links stay single-bracketed. See
250	[Todo format](notes/README.md#todo-format) for details.
251	
252	### Markdown anchor links
253	
254	GitHub anchor algorithm: lowercase, strip non-alphanumeric
255	characters in place, map remaining spaces to hyphens 1-for-1. Do
256	**not** collapse adjacent whitespace — so `a + b` → `a--b` (spaces
257	on both sides of `+`), but `a: b` → `a-b` (only trailing space on
258	`:`). General markdown reference:
259	[markdownguide.org](https://www.markdownguide.org). GitHub
```

> AGENT

Let's do it.

## The corrected review model — two gates, both *before* the commit

Per commit:
1. I do the work.
2. **Gate 1 — work review.** I say "ready to commit" and stop, *before writing any description*. You review the working-copy changes in Zed. This is where you'll most often request changes; we iterate until the work is right.
3. I write the commit title + body.
4. **Gate 2 — message review.** I show the description and stop; you review the message; we iterate.
5. I commit.

ESC-ESC and "stop on deviation/question" still apply on top.

This reverses what node 2 just landed — I wrote `### Commit-first review model` (commit, *then* review) and a `### User approval` that says "commit freely, no gate." Both are now wrong and get redone. Upside: the two-gate model is *shorter* to state than what node 2 wrote, so the correction also simplifies.

## On "overly complex" — my read

The problem isn't one bloated section, it's **fragmentation**. "How do I run one cycle / make one commit / push" is scattered across ~8 sections that overlap:

`## Committing` · `## Commit Message Style` · `### User approval` · `### Versioning` · `### Pre-commit checklist` · `## Cycle Protocol` · `## ochid Trailers` · `## Commit-Push-Finalize Flow`

The same rule shows up two and three places — push/squash in `### Versioning` *and* `## Commit-Push-Finalize`; ochid in `## ochid Trailers` *and* `## Cycle Protocol`; commit templates in `## Committing` *and* the flow. A reader has to assemble the flow from eight places.

**The big lever:** consolidate all of it into one linear section that reads top-to-bottom as the actual flow — version bump → work → Gate 1 → description → Gate 2 → commit → discretionary push — with templates, ochid, and the cargo cycle inline where they happen.

Two smaller candidates:
- The chores conventions are 3 separate sections — compressible to one.
- `## Commit-Push-Finalize Flow` carries a lot of `vc-x1 push` flag/stage/recovery detail that's really `vc-x1 push --help` material, not bot instructions.

Where does the complexity bite most for you — the cycle/commit scatter, the chores rules, the push-command detail, or something else? That tells me where to aim.

> DEVELOPER

> AGENT

Exactly — the Work phase grew from 1 to 2 commits because the work did, with no up-front "single vs multi" guess. Node 3 = `0.56.0-2` (the big lever); close-out shifts to `0.56.0-3`.

## Node 3 plan — one linear `## Cycle Protocol`

Rewritten to read top-to-bottom as the flow:

1. **intro** — one protocol, three phases, push/squash discretionary
2. **`### Numbering`** — the `.`-scheme, trailing-`0`, nesting, ordering caveat
3. **`### Preparation`** — version bump; pickup `## Todo` → `## In Progress`; chores opener mirrors
4. **`### Per-commit flow`** — cargo cycle → marker flip → **Gate 1 (work review)** → write description → **Gate 2 (message review)** → commit. Commit-message style, `jj commit` templates, and the ochid trailer inline here; ESC-ESC / deviation as overrides
5. **`### Close-out`** — `## In Progress` → `## Done`; finalize chores; squash-or-keep
6. **`### Pushing`** — discretionary; `vc-x1 push`; `.claude` commits per push; the hard-stop-after-finalize rule
7. **`### ochid trailers`** — the trailer format

**Folds in (sections that disappear):** `## Committing`, `## Commit Message Style`, `## ochid Trailers`, `## Commit-Push-Finalize Flow`, plus `### Versioning` / `### Pre-commit checklist` / `### User approval` leaving `## Pre-commit Requirements`.

**Cut to `vc-x1 push --help`:** the push stage machine, the `--from`/`--restart`/`--status`/`--dry-run` flag docs, manual-finalize fallback, late-changes recovery. CLAUDE.md keeps only what the *bot* must know — discretionary push, the two gates, the hard stop after finalize. That cut is the real size win.

**`## Pre-commit Requirements`** is then left with only notes-file conventions (refs, anchors, the 3 chores subsections) — I'd rename it `## Notes & Chores Conventions`. Compressing those 3 chores subsections is a separate smaller lever — I'll leave it out of node 3 unless you want it in.

The corrected two-gate review model lives in step 4.

One thing to confirm — it's the biggest deletion: **OK to cut the `vc-x1 push` flag/stage/recovery detail down to a pointer to `--help`?** On your yes I'll write node 3.

That's because node 2 got committed under the old commit-first model — right before you switched to review-before-commit. So node 1 and node 2 are both in (`ynunuxxo`, `xtwryyzn`) and `@` is empty. Node 1 you effectively reviewed — the chores-mirror fix was your feedback. Node 2 you never got to.

**Fix:** `jj undo` uncommits node 2 — its changes return to the working copy, uncommitted and reviewable in Zed.

**My recommendation — merge node 2 into node 3.** The big-lever consolidation rewrites the very sections node 2 produced (`### Versioning`, `## Cycle Protocol`, the review sections). Node 2 is really just scaffolding for node 3, and after the close-out squash it vanishes anyway. So:

1. `jj undo` node 2 → its changes sit uncommitted in `@`.
2. I do the big-lever consolidation on top.
3. You review the whole CLAUDE.md change in Zed — **Gate 1**.
4. I write the description — **Gate 2**.
5. One commit.

That puts the Work phase back at one commit — same flexibility principle, just pointing the other way now that we know node 3 supersedes node 2.

If you'd rather keep them separate: `jj undo` node 2, review it as-is, I recommit it under the two gates, then node 3 separately — but you'd be reviewing content node 3 is about to rewrite.

442	## Cycle Protocol
443	
444	The cycle — Preparation / Work-N / Close-out, with the
445	numbering and nesting defined in [Versioning](#versioning) —
446	runs by the mechanics below. They apply to every commit of
447	the cycle, at any nesting depth.
448	
449	[`notes/substep-protocol.md`](notes/substep-protocol.md) is a
450	longer-form companion (the validated close-out squash recipe,
451	recovery); it predates this rewrite and still uses the old
452	"sub-step" vocabulary — reconciling it is a follow-up. Revset
453	primitives the protocol leans on (`@`, `@-`, `..`, `::`,
454	prefix matching) are in
455	[`notes/jj-revsets.md`](notes/jj-revsets.md).
456	
457	### todo.md status flips
458	
459	The plan ladder in `notes/todo.md > ## In Progress` carries
460	`(current)` / `(done)` markers that flip on a fixed cadence:
461	
462	- **Start of a commit:** mark its ladder entry `(current)`
463	  as the first edit — before any code/doc work.
464	- **End of a commit:** flip `(current)` → `(done)` **before**
465	  the cargo cycle and the commit, so the commit captures the
466	  completed state.
467	
468	Each commit thus records "this entry is done"; the next
469	commit's first edit records "next entry starts."
470	
471	### Pre-commit cargo cycle
472	
473	Run before every commit (not just at close-out):
474	
475	1. `cargo fmt`
476	2. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`
477	3. `cargo test`
478	4. `cargo install --path . --locked`
479	5. (re-test if anything substantive changed)
480	
481	Keeps every commit buildable, so bisection works across the
482	cycle's stack.
483	
484	### Commit-first review model
485	
486	Review happens at each **commit** — the commit is the review
487	unit. There is no per-file checkpoint and no separate
488	"review before the commit block" pause. Per commit:
489	
490	1. Make the commit's changes.
491	2. Run the cargo cycle.
492	3. **Commit** — both repos, ochid trailers; no approval gate
493	   before committing. Local jj commits are mutable, so
494	   committing freely is safe.
495	4. Summarize the commit briefly in chat.
496	5. The user reviews the committed revision in their editor
497	   (full file context — see [Reviewing committed
498	   work](#reviewing-committed-work)).
499	6. The user iterates if needed; the bot squashes follow-ups
500	   into the commit via `jj squash --into @-` (and
501	   `jj describe @-` — plus the chores header and its anchor
502	   back-refs — if the title changes).
503	7. The user signals to advance to the next commit.
504	
505	Two things override the per-commit cadence:
506	
507	- **Deviation or question** — any time the work deviates
508	  from the agreed plan, or a question arises, stop and
509	  surface it; don't push through.
510	- **ESC-ESC** — the user can interrupt at any point to pull
511	  a review or question forward.
512	
513	The two-gate ceremony (review + message approval) still
514	applies to the **push** — it crosses the local→remote
515	boundary and warrants explicit approval.
516	
517	### Ochid trailers
518	
519	Every commit body carries `ochid:` trailer(s) linking it to
520	its counterpart(s) in the other repo. `.claude` commits once
521	per **push** (see [`.claude` cadence](#claude-cadence)), so:
522	
523	- **Code-side commits** each carry one
524	  `ochid: /.claude/<.claude-chid>` — the `.claude`
525	  working-copy change ID, stable until `.claude` is committed
526	  at push time.
527	- **The `.claude` commit** carries one `ochid: /<code-chid>`
528	  per code commit in that push: a single trailer when one
529	  code commit is pushed (the squashed case), a list when
530	  several are.
531	
532	Use `vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -L` to capture the change IDs
533	(first line app, second `.claude`). The multi-line `ochid:`
534	list, and its fork / multi-user generalization, are designed
535	in [`notes/forks-multi-user.md`](notes/forks-multi-user.md).
536	
537	### `.claude` cadence
538	
539	`.claude` commits **once per push**. The `.claude`
540	working-copy node accumulates session data across the cycle's
541	commits and is described + committed when a push happens —
542	one push, one `.claude` commit, paired with every code commit
543	in that push. Its change ID is stable from the first code
544	commit that references it through to that `.claude` commit
545	(working-copy snapshots, `jj describe`, and the finalize
546	`jj commit` all preserve the change ID), so the `ochid:`
547	trailers resolve across the push.
548	
549	This supersedes the older per-sub-step `.claude` cadence,
550	which paired each sub-step commit with its own `.claude`
551	commit to keep any sub-step promotable to its own push. With
552	push decoupled from the commit cadence, `.claude` simply
553	follows the push.
554	
555	**`.claude` is a linear journal — all session work lives on
556	`main`.** The repo has no need for parallel feature-branch
557	bookmarks to mirror app-side branches. When the app sits on
558	e.g. a feature branch, `.claude` still commits to `main`.
559	Cross-references between sides are carried by the `ochid:`
560	trailer + commit timestamps; that's enough to associate
561	session activity with whichever app-side branch the cycle was
562	on.
563	
564	**Do not create or maintain `.claude` bookmarks that mirror
565	app-side branches.** This was tried once during 0.41.1-6.7 on
566	the impression that an app-side fork needed a `.claude`
567	partner; the partner bookmark went unused for the cycle and
568	the work landed on `.claude main` regardless. Keeping such
569	bookmarks risks the bot misreading them as "this branch needs
570	to advance" and steering session pushes to the wrong remote
571	ref.
572	
573	### Cycle close-out — squash or keep separate?
574	
575	At close-out, two valid shapes for landing the cycle on its
576	target bookmark:
577	
578	- **Squash to one commit** — a single entry; per-commit
579	  granularity preserved only in the commit body's edit list.
580	  Right when the work is one logical change with intermediate
581	  validation points. **Cost:** the per-commit chores sections
582	  collapse into one, whose header becomes the close-out
583	  commit title, and every anchor back-ref to a now-gone
584	  section gets re-pointed — real work, done as part of the
585	  squash.
586	- **Keep separate** — one commit per cycle entry — when the
587	  decomposition is itself informative (distinct conceptual
588	  stages, design progression worth showing in `git log`).
589	  Used on `0.41.1-6.7` (8 commits + 1 close-out commit). Each
590	  chores section keeps its own header / `Commits:` ref — no
591	  consolidation churn.
592	
593	Pick at close-out, not at cycle start. No firm default —
594	weigh the squash's chores-consolidation cost against a
595	cleaner `git log`; that cost biases toward keeping separate
596	unless the decomposition genuinely isn't informative.
597	
598	### Reviewing committed work
599	
600	The commit-first model assumes the reviewer can read the diff
601	of an already-committed revision. Don't `jj edit -r @-` back
602	into a past commit to view it — that marks the commit mutable,
603	shifts the WC pointer, forces a `jj new -r <head>` recovery.
604	Use one of:
605	
606	**Terminal (always works):**
607	
608	```
609	jj diff -r @-                  # diff of previous commit
610	jj diff --from <X> --to <Y>    # diff between two arbitrary revs
611	jj show -r <X>                 # description + diff for one rev
612	jj log -r @-..@                # what's between two points
613	```
614	
615	Pipe through `delta` / `diff-so-fancy | less -R` for color and
616	side-by-side.
617	
618	**External diff tool** — configure jj to launch your editor:
619	
620	```
621	# ~/.config/jj/config.toml
622	[ui]
623	diff-editor = ["zed", "--diff", "$left", "$right"]
624	```
625	
626	Then `jj diff -r @-` opens the configured tool. Works for
627	arbitrary `--from`/`--to` ranges.
628	
629	**VS Code** — Source Control panel → Commit Graph →
630	right-click commit A → "Copy Commit ID" → right-click commit
631	B → "Compare with…" → paste / pick A. Two-commit diff opens
632	with the changed-files list. GitLens extension adds richer
633	"Open Comparison" actions; `Git: Compare with…` in the
634	Command Palette is a fallback.
635	
636	## Code Conventions
637	
```

No firm default —\nweigh the squash's chores-consolidation cost against a\ncleaner `git log`; that cost biases toward keeping separate\nunless the decomposition genuinely isn't informative.\n\n### Reviewing committed work\n\nThe commit-first model assumes the reviewer can read the diff\nof an already-committed revision. Don't `jj edit -r @-` back\ninto a past commit to view it — that marks the commit mutable,\nshifts the WC pointer, forces a `jj new -r <head>` recovery.\nUse one of:\n\n**Terminal (always works):**\n\n```\njj diff -r @-                  # diff of previous commit\njj diff --from <X> --to <Y>    # diff between two arbitrary revs\njj show -r <X>                 # description + diff for one rev\njj log -r @-..@                # what's between two points\n```\n\nPipe through `delta` / `diff-so-fancy | less -R` for color and\nside-by-side.\n\n**External diff tool** — configure jj to launch your editor:\n\n```\n# ~/.config/jj/config.toml\n[ui]\ndiff-editor = [\"zed\", \"--diff\", \"$left\", \"$right\"]\n```\n\nThen `jj diff -r @-` opens the configured tool. Works for\narbitrary `--from`/`--to` ranges.\n\n**VS Code** — Source Control panel → Commit Graph →\nright-click commit A → \"Copy Commit ID\" → right-click commit\nB → \"Compare with…\" → paste / pick A. Two-commit diff opens\nwith the changed-files list. GitLens extension adds richer\n\"Open Comparison\" actions; `Git: Compare with…` in the\nCommand Palette is a fallback.",
  "new_string": "## Cycle Protocol\n\nEvery change runs as a **cycle**: Preparation → Work-N →\nClose-out. One protocol; push and squash are actions you take\nwhen wanted, not modes chosen up front. The mechanics below\nrun in the order they happen.\n\n### Numbering\n\nThe version suffix on each commit carries its phase, with one\nseparator — `.` — at every depth (semver mandates `-` once at\nthe start of the prerelease, then `.` for each level after):\n\n- `X.Y.Z-0` — Preparation\n- `X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, … — Work commits\n- `X.Y.Z` — Close-out (bare version, no suffix)\n\nA **trailing `0` identifier marks a Preparation**, and the\nrule nests to any depth: when a Work commit needs subdividing\ninto its own sub-cycle, append another level — `X.Y.Z-3.0`\n(Preparation of the `-3` sub-cycle), `X.Y.Z-3.1` /\n`X.Y.Z-3.2` (its Work), `X.Y.Z-3` (its Close-out),\n`X.Y.Z-3.1.0` (Preparation of the `-3.1` sub-sub-cycle), and\nso on. A sub-cycle needing no Preparation just omits the `.0`\n(`-3.1`, `-3.2`, `-3`); one that grows a Preparation later\nadds `-3.0` with no renumbering of siblings.\n\nEach commit carries its phase version in its title **and**\n`Cargo.toml` — bump `Cargo.toml` at the start of each phase,\nso `vc-x1 -V` shows the active phase at build time.\n\n**Ordering caveat.** The one ordering guarantee semver gives\nis that the bare `X.Y.Z` close-out outranks every `X.Y.Z-…`\ndev version. Within the ladder it does *not* hold for nested\nclose-outs — `X.Y.Z-3` sorts *before* its children\n`X.Y.Z-3.0` / `X.Y.Z-3.1`, because a prefix always precedes\nits extensions. Cosmetic — nothing depends on within-ladder\norder — but don't claim monotonicity.\n\n**Why numeric suffixes** (`-0`, `-1`, …) rather than `-devN`:\nsemver pre-release identifiers compare per spec and Cargo\naccepts them. `-dev` adds no information the git log doesn't\ncarry and doubles typing per commit. The final close-out\ncommit (bare `X.Y.Z`) signals completion rather than amending\nprior commits — history stays readable, exploratory vs final\ncommits obvious.\n\n### Preparation\n\nThe cycle's first commit (`X.Y.Z-0`):\n\n- **Backfill the previous cycle's chores `Commits:` ref.**\n  Grab the just-pushed commit's URL + full SHA, fill in the\n  newest chores section that was left `Commits:`-less. See\n  [Chores commit references](#chores-commit-references).\n- **Bump the version** in `Cargo.toml` to `X.Y.Z-0`.\n- **Pick up a `## Todo` item** (if the cycle has one). Move\n  that entry's text out of `## Todo` and into `## In\n  Progress` — its overview and to-do list, followed by the\n  plan ladder — so `## In Progress` is self-describing, not\n  a bare ladder. A cycle that merges several entangled\n  `## Todo` items moves all of them. A `## Todo` entry that\n  duplicates current `## In Progress` work is a process bug.\n- **Open the chores section.** Header = provisional commit\n  title; intro that **mirrors** the `## In Progress` content\n  so the cycle stays readable once `## In Progress` is\n  cleared at close-out; any `###` design subsections. No\n  per-file edit list — that lives in the commit body.\n\n### Per-commit flow\n\nEvery commit (Preparation, each Work commit, Close-out) goes\nthrough:\n\n1. **Mark this commit `(current)`** as the first edit in\n   `notes/todo.md > ## In Progress`, before any code/doc\n   work.\n2. **Do the work.**\n3. **Flip this commit `(current)` → `(done)`** in `## In\n   Progress` — before the cargo cycle and the commit, so\n   the commit captures the completed state.\n4. **Cargo cycle** (skip-able for purely-docs commits; full\n   cycle mandatory at close-out):\n   1. `cargo fmt`\n   2. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`\n   3. `cargo test`\n   4. `cargo install --path . --locked`\n   5. (re-test if anything substantive changed)\n5. **Gate 1 — work review.** Stop *before* writing any\n   description; tell the user \"ready to commit.\" The user\n   reviews the working-copy changes in their editor. Most\n   change requests come here — iterate until the work is\n   right.\n6. **Write the commit description.** [Conventional\n   Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) with a\n   version suffix:\n\n   ```\n   <type>: <short description> (<version>)\n   ```\n\n   - **Title**: ≤50 chars. Common types: `feat`, `fix`,\n     `refactor`, `test`, `docs`, `chore`. The version\n     suffix counts toward the 50 — favor terse phrasings.\n   - **Body**: [Prose form](#prose-form) (intro + bullets),\n     wrap ≤72. App-repo bullets are **file-by-file** — one\n     bullet per file changed, file plus a one-line gist.\n     That list is the source of truth for the cycle's\n     mechanical change record; chores carries the narrative\n     + design, not a copy of it. Promote any \"why\" beyond\n     one sentence to a chores `###` subsection.\n   - **`ochid:` trailer** as the last line of the body —\n     see [ochid trailers](#ochid-trailers).\n   - **Session-repo (`.claude`) body**: same shape; bullets\n     describe in-session activity rather than code changes.\n7. **Gate 2 — message review.** Show the title + body and\n   stop. The user reviews the description. Iterate.\n8. **Commit.** `jj commit -m \"title\" -m \"body\" -R .` for\n   the app repo, `-R .claude` for the bot repo (`-R` last\n   keeps the verb visible):\n\n   ```\n   jj commit -m \\\n   \"<type>: <short description> (<version>)\" \\\n   -m \"<intro paragraph>\n\n   - file1: gist\n   - file2: gist\n\n   ochid: /.claude/<chid>\" \\\n   -R .\n   ```\n\n**Two overrides apply:**\n\n- **Deviation or question** — any time the work deviates\n  from the agreed plan, or a question arises, stop and\n  surface it; don't push through.\n- **ESC-ESC** — the user can interrupt at any point to pull\n  a review or question forward.\n\n### Close-out\n\nThe cycle's last commit (bare `X.Y.Z`):\n\n- **Move the picked-up item** from `## In Progress` to a\n  one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) in `## Done`.\n- **Finalize the chores section.** Sync the section header\n  to the **final** commit title and update every markdown\n  anchor back-reference to it; add an `### As-built ladder`\n  listing the cycle's commits; `### Outcome` notes if\n  useful.\n- **Decide squash or keep separate.** Two valid shapes:\n  - **Squash to one commit** — single entry on the target;\n    per-commit granularity preserved only in the commit\n    body's edit list. Right when the work is one logical\n    change with intermediate validation points. Cost: the\n    per-commit chores sections collapse into one (its\n    header becomes the close-out commit title), and every\n    anchor back-ref to a now-gone section gets re-pointed\n    — real work, done as part of the squash.\n  - **Keep separate** — one commit per cycle entry — when\n    the decomposition is itself informative (distinct\n    conceptual stages worth showing in `git log`). Each\n    chores section keeps its own header / `Commits:` ref;\n    no consolidation churn.\n- **Update `notes/README.md`** if functionality changed\n  (new flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).\n\n### Pushing\n\n**Push is discretionary** — done when you want to back up\nwork or publish interim progress, and **always at\nclose-out** (the cycle's result must be published). Interim\npushes are a judgment call; nothing forces push-per-commit.\n\nThe flow is wrapped by `vc-x1 push <bookmark>`, which runs:\n\n1. Preflight (`vc-x1 sync --check` + `cargo fmt` / `clippy`\n   / `test`).\n2. **Review-gate prompt** — shows `jj diff --stat` for both\n   repos and asks `[y/N]`.\n3. **Message-gate prompt** — uses `--title` / `--body` or\n   opens `$EDITOR`.\n4. Commits both repos with ochid trailers, advances the\n   bookmark, `jj git push --bookmark <bookmark>` for the\n   app, finalizes `.claude` (its single per-push commit).\n\n`<bookmark>` is the **app-side working branch** (often\n`main`, sometimes a feature branch). `.claude` always pushes\nits `main` regardless of the app-side bookmark. See\n`vc-x1 push --help` for the full flag list, resumability\n(`--from`, `--restart`, `--status`), and recovery; CLAUDE.md\nkeeps only the rules that govern bot behavior. `vc-x1 sync\n--check` may also be run manually before editing to surface\nremote changes early.\n\n**`.claude` cadence — once per push.** The `.claude`\nworking-copy node accumulates session data across the\ncycle's commits and is described + committed when a push\nhappens — one push, one `.claude` commit, paired with every\ncode commit in that push. Its change ID is stable from the\nfirst code commit that references it through to that\n`.claude` commit (working-copy snapshots, `jj describe`, and\nthe finalize `jj commit` all preserve the change ID), so the\n`ochid:` trailers resolve across the push.\n\n`.claude` is a linear journal — all session work lives on\n`main`. The repo has no need for parallel feature-branch\nbookmarks to mirror app-side branches; when the app sits on\na feature branch, `.claude` still commits to `main`.\nCross-references between sides are carried by the `ochid:`\ntrailer + commit timestamps; that is enough. **Do not\ncreate or maintain `.claude` bookmarks that mirror app-side\nbranches** — tried during 0.41.1-6.7, went unused, and\nrisks the bot steering session pushes to the wrong remote\nref.\n\n**Bot communication at the gates.** Use plain prose — no\ninsider jargon (\"Gate N signal\", \"Checkpoint N\", etc.):\n\n- **At work-review** — summarize what changed and stop.\n  \"Work complete. Please review.\"\n- **At message-review** — present `$TITLE` and `$BODY`\n  explicitly, then ask permission to commit (or push).\n  Don't spell out the full\n  `vc-x1 push <bookmark> --yes --title \"$TITLE\" --body\n  \"$BODY\"` invocation by default; it's mechanical and\n  obvious from title/body. Show it only on explicit\n  request.\n\n**After finalize: stop and wait.** After `vc-x1 finalize`\nlaunches — whether mid-session per-push or at session-end —\nyou **MUST NEVER** proceed to a next step, edit files, run\ntools, or emit any text (prose, recaps, acknowledgements),\nuntil the user explicitly directs you to continue. Treat\nfinalize as a hard stop for the whole turn. Any final words\nmust be said in the approval prompt *before* executing\nfinalize; the finalize `Bash` call is the last thing in the\nturn and nothing follows it.\n\nThis holds even when the next step seems obvious. Wait. The\nuser controls cadence — every push+finalize is a checkpoint\nthey may want to inspect, think about, hand off, or take a\nbreak at. Auto-proceeding bypasses that checkpoint.\n\nIf push exits before `finalize-claude` (e.g. failure between\n`push-app` and `finalize-claude`), run finalize by hand:\n\n```\nvc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log\n```\n\nClear push's saved state after any out-of-band recovery —\n`rm .vc-x1/push-state.toml` or `vc-x1 push <bookmark>\n--restart` — otherwise push resumes from a stale stage.\n\nA late code-repo tweak after `push-app` succeeded (e.g.\nupdating CLAUDE.md or memory) requires `jj squash\n--ignore-immutable` and a re-push; that is a remote rewrite\nand needs explicit approval like any push.\n\n### ochid trailers\n\nEvery commit body carries `ochid:` trailer(s) linking it to\nits counterpart(s) in the other repo. The path is\nworkspace-root-relative — `/.claude/<chid>` from the app\nside, `/<chid>` from the `.claude` side.\n\nBecause `.claude` commits once per push:\n\n- **Code-side commits** each carry one\n  `ochid: /.claude/<.claude-chid>` — the `.claude`\n  working-copy change ID, stable until `.claude` is\n  committed at push time.\n- **The `.claude` commit** carries one `ochid: /<code-chid>`\n  per code commit in that push: a single trailer when one\n  code commit is pushed (the squashed case), a list when\n  several are. The multi-line `ochid:` list and its\n  fork / multi-user generalization are designed in\n  [`notes/forks-multi-user.md`](notes/forks-multi-user.md).\n\nUse `vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -L` to capture the change IDs\n(first line app, second `.claude`).\n\n### Reviewing changes\n\nThe two-gate model reviews **uncommitted working-copy\nchanges** in your editor, on the way to commit. Zed shows\nthe diff naturally; for terminal use:\n\n```\njj diff                          # working-copy diff (uncommitted)\njj diff -r @-                    # diff of the previous commit\njj diff --from <X> --to <Y>      # any two revisions\njj show -r <X>                   # description + diff for one rev\n```\n\nPipe through `delta` or `diff-so-fancy | less -R` for color\nand side-by-side. To launch your editor as the diff tool:\n\n```\n# ~/.config/jj/config.toml\n[ui]\ndiff-editor = [\"zed\", \"--diff\", \"$left\", \"$right\"]\n```\n\nThen `jj diff --from X --to Y` opens it. Don't `jj edit\n-r @-` to view a past commit — that marks it mutable and\nshifts the working-copy pointer; use `jj diff -r @-` or\n`jj show -r @-`.\n\n[`notes/substep-protocol.md`](notes/substep-protocol.md) is\na longer-form companion (close-out squash recipe, recovery)\nthat predates this rewrite and still uses the old \"sub-step\"\nvocabulary — reconciling it is a follow-up. Revset\nprimitives (`@`, `@-`, `..`, `::`, prefix matching) are in\n[`notes/jj-revsets.md`](notes/jj-revsets.md)."
}

69	## Committing
70	
71	Use `-R` (`--repository`) at the end to target the correct repo. Use
72	relative paths to reduce noise. Putting `-R` last keeps the verb/action
73	visible at the start of the command.
74	
75	### App repo
76	```
77	jj commit -m \
78	"title" \
79	-m "body
80	
81	ochid: /.claude/<changeID>" \
82	-R .
83	```
84	
85	### Bot session repo
86	```
87	jj commit -m \
88	"title" \
89	-m "body
90	
91	ochid: /<changeID>" \
92	-R .claude
93	```

141	## Commit Message Style
142	
143	Use [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) with
144	a version suffix:
145	
146	```
147	<type>: <short description> (<version>)
148	```
149	
150	- **Title**: ≤50 chars (the "50" of the git 50/72 rule), a short
151	  summary of *what* changed. The version suffix counts toward the
152	  50 — the descriptive part gets whatever's left after `<type>: `
153	  and ` (<version>)`, so favor terse phrasings (`port X to Context`
154	  over `X → Context + XParams`) when the names run long. Common
155	  types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `test`, `docs`, `chore`.
156	- **Body**: [Prose form](#prose-form) shape (intro + bullets), wrap
157	  ≤72 (the "72" of the 50/72 rule). App-repo bullets are
158	  **file-by-file** — one bullet per file changed, file plus a
159	  one-line gist. This list **is the source of truth** for the
160	  cycle's mechanical change record; chores carries the narrative +
161	  design, not a copy of it. Promote any "why" beyond one sentence
162	  to a chores `###` subsection rather than expanding the intro.
163	- **Session-repo body**: same shape; bullets describe in-session
164	  activity rather than code changes.
165	- Examples:
166	  - `feat: add fix-ochid subcommand (0.22.0)`
167	  - `fix: fix-ochid prefix bug (0.22.1)`
168	  - `refactor: deduplicate common CLI flags (0.21.1)`
169	
170	## Pre-commit Requirements
171	
172	### User approval
173	
174	Local jj commits are mutable, so the bot commits and squashes
175	**freely** under the [commit-first review
176	model](#commit-first-review-model) — no approval gate before a
177	commit; review happens *after* each commit on the committed
178	revision (with deviation / question / ESC-ESC as the
179	overrides).
180	
181	**push and finalize cross the local→remote boundary and
182	require explicit user approval** — the two-gate ceremony
183	(review + message). Never run `vc-x1 push`, `jj git push`, or
184	`vc-x1 finalize` without it. A late squash into an
185	already-pushed commit (`jj squash --ignore-immutable`) is a
186	remote rewrite and needs approval too.
187	
188	### Notes references

209	### Versioning
210	
211	Every plan starts with a version bump, and runs as one
212	**cycle** with three phases:
213	
214	- **Preparation** — bump the version; populate `## In
215	  Progress` with the cycle's overview and plan (moving in a
216	  `## Todo` item if the cycle picks one up); open the chores
217	  section.
218	- **Work-N** — the implementation, in one or more commits.
219	- **Close-out** — move the `## In Progress` item to a
220	  `## Done` one-liner; finalize the chores section.
221	
222	There is one protocol — this. "Single-step" / "multi-step"
223	are no longer separate protocols; a small change is just a
224	cycle with few Work commits.
225	
226	**Pickup and close-out.** A plan that picks up a `## Todo`
227	item — or *re-scopes* an existing `## In Progress` ladder to
228	absorb one — **moves that entry into `## In Progress`** in
229	the Preparation commit:
230	
231	- **Pickup:** the entry's text moves out of `## Todo` and
232	  into `## In Progress` — its overview and to-do list,
233	  followed by the plan ladder — so the section is
234	  self-describing, not a bare ladder. The chores opener
235	  **mirrors** that content so the cycle stays readable once
236	  `## In Progress` is cleared.
237	- **Close-out:** the item is removed from `## In Progress`
238	  and a one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) is added to
239	  `## Done`.
240	
241	`## In Progress` is the sole live record of the work between
242	those points; the chores opener is the durable capture. A
243	cycle that merges several entangled `## Todo` items moves
244	all of them. A `## Todo` entry that duplicates current
245	`## In Progress` work is a process bug.
246	
247	**Numbering.** The version suffix carries the phase, with
248	one separator — `.` — at every depth (semver mandates `-`
249	once at the start of the prerelease, then `.` for each level
250	after):
251	
252	- `X.Y.Z-0` — Preparation
253	- `X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, … — Work commits
254	- `X.Y.Z` — Close-out (bare version, no suffix)
255	
256	A **trailing `0` identifier marks a Preparation**, and the
257	rule nests to any depth: when a Work commit needs
258	subdividing into its own sub-cycle, append another level —
259	`X.Y.Z-3.0` (Preparation of the `-3` sub-cycle), `X.Y.Z-3.1`
260	/ `X.Y.Z-3.2` (its Work), `X.Y.Z-3` (its Close-out),
261	`X.Y.Z-3.1.0` (Preparation of the `-3.1` sub-sub-cycle), and
262	so on. A sub-cycle needing no Preparation just omits the
263	`.0` (`-3.1`, `-3.2`, `-3`); one that grows a Preparation
264	later adds `-3.0` with no renumbering of siblings.
265	
266	Each commit carries its phase version in its title **and**
267	`Cargo.toml` — bump `Cargo.toml` at the start of each phase,
268	so `vc-x1 -V` shows the active phase at build time.
269	
270	**Ordering caveat.** The one ordering guarantee semver gives
271	is that the bare `X.Y.Z` close-out outranks every `X.Y.Z-…`
272	dev version. Within the ladder it does *not* hold for nested
273	close-outs — `X.Y.Z-3` sorts *before* its children
274	`X.Y.Z-3.0` / `X.Y.Z-3.1`, because a prefix always precedes
275	its extensions. Cosmetic — nothing depends on within-ladder
276	order — but don't claim monotonicity.
277	
278	**Why numeric suffixes** (`-0`, `-1`, …) rather than
279	`-devN`: semver pre-release identifiers compare per spec and
280	Cargo accepts them. `-dev` adds no information the git log
281	doesn't carry and doubles typing per commit. The final
282	close-out commit (bare `X.Y.Z`) signals completion rather
283	than amending prior commits — history stays readable,
284	exploratory vs final commits obvious.
285	
286	**push and squash are discretionary.** A cycle's commits
287	accumulate locally; **push** and **squash** are not modes
288	fixed at cycle start but actions taken when wanted:
289	
290	- **push** — to back up work or to publish interim
291	  progress. Interim pushes are a judgement call; the
292	  **close-out push is mandatory** — a finished cycle must be
293	  published.
294	- **squash** — to tidy history, typically collapsing the
295	  cycle's commits into one before a publishing push. Whether
296	  to squash is decided at close-out, not cycle start.
297	
298	The cycle *mechanics* — per-commit cargo cycle, the
299	commit-first review model, ochid trailers, `.claude`
300	cadence, the squash recipe, reviewing committed work — are
301	in [Cycle Protocol](#cycle-protocol).
302	
303	### Headings and entries that record a commit
```

405	### Pre-commit checklist
406	
407	At cycle *start* (before the version bump): (a) backfill the
408	previous chores section's `Commits:` ref with the just-pushed
409	commit's URL + full SHA — see
410	[Chores commit references](#chores-commit-references); (b) if
411	this cycle picks up a `## Todo` item (or re-scopes `## In
412	Progress` to absorb one), move that `## Todo` entry's text
413	into `## In Progress` and mirror it in the chores opener —
414	see [Versioning](#versioning).
415	
416	Before each commit, run all of the following and fix any issues:
417	
418	1. `cargo fmt`
419	2. `cargo clippy`
420	3. `cargo test`
421	4. `cargo install --path . --locked` (if applicable) — `--locked`
422	   is required: without it, `cargo install` ignores `Cargo.lock`
423	   and re-resolves from scratch, which can pick incompatible
424	   versions even when `cargo build` / `cargo test` succeed.
425	5. Retest after install
426	6. Update `notes/todo.md` — flip the just-completed commit's
427	   ladder marker `(current)` → `(done)` **before** the commit
428	   (see [todo.md status flips](#todomd-status-flips)). The
429	   next commit's `(current)` marker is set at its start.
430	7. Update `notes/todo.md` — at cycle close-out (final commit),
431	   remove the picked-up item from `## In Progress` and add a
432	   one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) to `## Done`.
433	8. Update `notes/chores/chores-*.md` — add a section (header =
434	   provisional commit title; intro paragraph + any `###` design
435	   subsections; **no** per-file edit list — that's the commit
436	   body).
437	9. Sync the chores section header to the **final** commit title
438	   and update every markdown anchor back-reference to it.
439	10. Update `notes/README.md` — if functionality changed (new
440	    flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).
441	
442	## Cycle Protocol

835	## ochid Trailers
836	
837	Every commit body must include an `ochid:` trailer pointing to the
838	counterpart commit in the other repo. The value is a workspace-root-relative
839	path followed by the changeID:
840	
841	- App repo commits point to `.claude`: `ochid: /.claude/<changeID>`
842	- Bot session commits point to app repo: `ochid: /<changeID>`
843	
844	Use `vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -L` to get both changeIDs (first line
845	is app repo, second is `.claude`).
846	
847	## Commit-Push-Finalize Flow
848	
849	**Use `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` — it wraps the full flow in one
850	command with two interactive approval gates.** Push runs
851	preflight (sync + fmt + clippy + test), prompts for review,
852	composes the commit message (via `--title`/`--body` or `$EDITOR`),
853	prompts for message approval, then commits both repos → advances
854	bookmarks → pushes app → finalizes `.claude`. Failures inside the
855	local mutation window roll both repos back via `jj op restore`;
856	after `push-app` succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and
857	recovery is forward-only.
858	
859	**Run this flow when you push** — push is discretionary: to
860	back up work or to publish interim progress, and always at
861	close-out (see [Versioning](#versioning) on push timing). A
862	single push can carry one commit or a run of them.
863	
864	### Run `vc-x1 push`
865	
866	`<bookmark>` below is the **app-side working branch** — the
867	bookmark sitting at the tip of the chain `vc-x1 push` should
868	advance and push. For most cycles this is `main`; for
869	feature-branch work it's the feature-branch name (e.g.
870	`init-clone-refactor`). Pass the literal name, not the
871	placeholder. **`.claude` always pushes its `main`** regardless
872	of the app-side bookmark passed (see
873	[`.claude` cadence](#claude-cadence) — `.claude` is a linear
874	journal).
875	
876	```
877	vc-x1 push <bookmark>                                  # interactive (review + $EDITOR)
878	vc-x1 push <bookmark> --title "..." --body "..."       # flags skip $EDITOR
879	vc-x1 push <bookmark> --yes --title "..." --body "..." # full non-interactive
880	vc-x1 push <bookmark> --dry-run                        # preview (no side effects)
881	vc-x1 push <bookmark> --from commit-app                # resume from specific stage
882	vc-x1 push --status                                    # show saved state
883	vc-x1 push <bookmark> --restart                        # clear saved state; start fresh
884	```
885	
886	**Bookmark mismatch is currently silent.** `vc-x1 push`
887	doesn't verify that `<bookmark>` matches the working-copy
888	chain — passing a bookmark whose tip isn't an ancestor of `@`
889	will (silently) push that bookmark anyway, leaving your work
890	unpushed. Always confirm `jj log -r <bookmark>..@` shows the
891	commits you mean to send. A safety check is on the 0.42.0
892	todo (`vc-x1 push: --scope` flag item).
893	
894	The two approval gates are surfaced by push itself:
895	
896	1. **Review** — push prints `jj diff --stat` for both repos and
897	   prompts `[y/N]`. Approve = "the work is done right".
898	2. **Message** — push either uses `--title`/`--body` (non-editor
899	   path) or opens `$EDITOR` on a template. Approve = "the message
900	   reads right".
901	
902	Both titles and bodies are the **same** across the two commits;
903	only the `ochid:` trailer differs per repo. Push collects the
904	pre-commit chids internally so you don't hand-manage them.
905	
906	For the full flag list and stage machine, see `vc-x1 push --help`
907	and `notes/chores/chores-05.md > Add push subcommand (0.37.0)`.
908	
909	### Bot communication during the flow
910	
911	When applying the flow on the user's behalf, use plain prose at
912	each gate — no insider jargon ("Gate N signal", "Checkpoint N",
913	etc.):
914	
915	1. **After completing the work** — summarize what changed
916	   (file-by-file or feature-by-feature, terse) and end with
917	   something like:
918	
919	   > Work complete. Please review. On approval I'll prep the
920	   > commit title and body for a second approval before pushing.
921	
922	2. **After review approval** — present the proposed `$TITLE` and
923	   `$BODY` explicitly, then ask permission to run `vc-x1 push`.
924	   Do **not** spell out the full
925	   `vc-x1 push <bookmark> --yes --title "$TITLE" --body "$BODY"`
926	   invocation by default — it's mechanical and obvious from the
927	   title/body. Show it only on explicit request (verbose mode /
928	   debugging).
929	
930	3. **After execution approval** — run the push command. `push`
931	   handles commit + bookmark + push + finalize internally;
932	   nothing should be output after the push command (finalize is
933	   detached and absolute-last; see "After finalize: stop and
934	   wait" below).
935	
936	### Pre-step: `vc-x1 sync` (still useful)
937	
938	Push's preflight runs `vc-x1 sync --check` as its first step. Check
939	mode verifies divergence without applying — if anything is `behind`
940	or `diverged`, preflight errors and you resolve explicitly with
941	`vc-x1 sync --no-check` before re-running push. This keeps the
942	two approval gates (review, message) authoritative; push never
943	performs an unsupervised rebase.
944	
945	Running sync manually before you *start* editing is still cheap
946	(one line when clean) and surfaces remote changes earlier:
947	
948	```
949	vc-x1 sync --check                    # dual-repo workspace, verify
950	vc-x1 sync --no-check                 # apply (rebase / fast-forward)
951	vc-x1 sync --check -R .               # single-repo project
952	vc-x1 sync --check --quiet            # silent; exit code signals result
953	```
954	
955	Bots and scripts must pass `--check` or `--no-check` explicitly —
956	defaults can shift, explicit flags lock in the contract. Interactive
957	use can take the default (which is `--check`).
958	
959	Output shape:
960	
961	- **Clean**: one line — `sync: N repos, all bookmarks up-to-date`.
962	  Note: scope is bookmark-vs-remote tracking, not working-copy
963	  cleanliness — `@` may have uncommitted changes; sync doesn't
964	  speak to that. Proceed.
965	- **Action needed** (`behind` / `diverged`) under `--check`:
966	  per-repo fetch + state lines, then a fatal
967	  `sync: N repos need action — resolve with vc-x1 sync --no-check
968	  and re-run`. Inspect, run `vc-x1 sync --no-check`, then proceed.
969	- **`--quiet`**: no output; exit code is the only signal.
970	
971	### After finalize: stop and wait
972	
973	After `vc-x1 finalize` is launched — **whether mid-session per-step
974	or at session end** — you **MUST NEVER** proceed to a next step, edit
975	files, run tools, or emit any text (prose, recaps, acknowledgements),
976	until the user explicitly directs you to continue. Treat finalize as
977	a hard stop for the whole turn. Any final words (e.g. "next is ...")
978	must be said in the approval prompt *before* executing finalize; the
979	finalize `Bash` call is the last thing in the turn and nothing
980	follows it.
981	
982	This holds even when the next step seems obvious (e.g. "next is
983	N+1" or "now I should bump the version and commit the release").
984	Wait. The user controls cadence — every push+finalize is a checkpoint
985	they may want to inspect, think about, hand off, or take a break at.
986	Auto-proceeding bypasses that checkpoint and produces unwanted writes
987	between finalize and the next explicit instruction.
988	
989	Exceptions to this rule may emerge later but are not authorized at
990	this stage. Until told otherwise, treat as absolute.
991	
992	### Late changes after push
993	
994	If the app repo needs a tweak after `push-app` succeeded (e.g.
995	updating CLAUDE.md or memory), the commit is immutable. Use
996	`--ignore-immutable` to squash the changes into `@-` — the
997	bookmark moves with the rewritten commit — then re-push:
998	
999	```
1000	jj squash --ignore-immutable -R .
1001	jj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R .
1002	```
1003	
1004	If you squash somewhere other than `@-`, add
1005	`jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r <target> -R .` between those
1006	two commands so the bookmark lands on the rewritten commit.
1007	
1008	`.claude` is also mutable via this pattern when needed, though
1009	push's `finalize-claude` stage normally handles trailing session
1010	writes so you rarely hit this case there.
1011	
1012	### Manual finalize fallback
1013	
1014	If push exited before `finalize-claude` (e.g. `--no-finalize`
1015	was set, or a failure between `push-app` and `finalize-claude`),
1016	run finalize by hand:
1017	
1018	```
1019	vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
1020	```
1021	
1022	**Nothing should happen after finalize** — no memory writes, no
1023	tool calls, no additional output. If any work is done after
1024	finalize, run finalize again so the trailing writes are captured.
1025	Do **not** echo or restate the finalize output — the Bash tool
1026	already displays it.
1027	
1028	**Clear push's saved state after any out-of-band recovery** —
1029	manual `vc-x1 finalize`, manual `jj squash --ignore-immutable`
1030	+ force-push, etc., all leave `.vc-x1/push-state.toml` pointing
1031	at a now-stale halt point. Either `rm .vc-x1/push-state.toml`
1032	or run `vc-x1 push <bookmark> --restart` (which clears and
1033	restarts in one go) before the next `vc-x1 push`. Otherwise
1034	push resumes from a bogus stage and can falsely declare
```

The version suffix counts toward the\n  50 — the descriptive part gets whatever's left after `<type>: `\n  and ` (<version>)`, so favor terse phrasings (`port X to Context`\n  over `X → Context + XParams`) when the names run long. Common\n  types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `test`, `docs`, `chore`.\n- **Body**: [Prose form](#prose-form) shape (intro + bullets), wrap\n  ≤72 (the \"72\" of the 50/72 rule). App-repo bullets are\n  **file-by-file** — one bullet per file changed, file plus a\n  one-line gist. This list **is the source of truth** for the\n  cycle's mechanical change record; chores carries the narrative +\n  design, not a copy of it. Promote any \"why\" beyond one sentence\n  to a chores `###` subsection rather than expanding the intro.\n- **Session-repo body**: same shape; bullets describe in-session\n  activity rather than code changes.\n- Examples:\n  - `feat: add fix-ochid subcommand (0.22.0)`\n  - `fix: fix-ochid prefix bug (0.22.1)`\n  - `refactor: deduplicate common CLI flags (0.21.1)`\n\n## Pre-commit Requirements",

The chores opener\n  **mirrors** that content so the cycle stays readable once\n  `## In Progress` is cleared.\n- **Close-out:** the item is removed from `## In Progress`\n  and a one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) is added to\n  `## Done`.\n\n`## In Progress` is the sole live record of the work between\nthose points; the chores opener is the durable capture. A\ncycle that merges several entangled `## Todo` items moves\nall of them. A `## Todo` entry that duplicates current\n`## In Progress` work is a process bug.\n\n**Numbering.** The version suffix carries the phase, with\none separator — `.` — at every depth (semver mandates `-`\nonce at the start of the prerelease, then `.` for each level\nafter):\n\n- `X.Y.Z-0` — Preparation\n- `X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, … — Work commits\n- `X.Y.Z` — Close-out (bare version, no suffix)\n\nA **trailing `0` identifier marks a Preparation**, and the\nrule nests to any depth: when a Work commit needs\nsubdividing into its own sub-cycle, append another level —\n`X.Y.Z-3.0` (Preparation of the `-3` sub-cycle), `X.Y.Z-3.1`\n/ `X.Y.Z-3.2` (its Work), `X.Y.Z-3` (its Close-out),\n`X.Y.Z-3.1.0` (Preparation of the `-3.1` sub-sub-cycle), and\nso on. Update `notes/chores/chores-*.md` — add a section (header =\n   provisional commit title; intro paragraph + any `###` design\n   subsections; **no** per-file edit list — that's the commit\n   body).\n9. Sync the chores section header to the **final** commit title\n   and update every markdown anchor back-reference to it.\n10. Update `notes/README.md` — if functionality changed (new\n    flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).\n\n## Cycle Protocol",
  "new_string": "## Cycle Protocol"
}
