```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/CLAUDE.md",
  "old_string": "## ochid Trailers\n\nEvery commit body must include an `ochid:` trailer pointing to the\ncounterpart commit in the other repo. The value is a workspace-root-relative\npath followed by the changeID:\n\n- App repo commits point to `.claude`: `ochid: /.claude/<changeID>`\n- Bot session commits point to app repo: `ochid: /<changeID>`\n\nUse `vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -L` to get both changeIDs (first line\nis app repo, second is `.claude`).\n\n## Commit-Push-Finalize Flow\n\n**Use `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` — it wraps the full flow in one\ncommand with two interactive approval gates.** Push runs\npreflight (sync + fmt + clippy + test), prompts for review,\ncomposes the commit message (via `--title`/`--body` or `$EDITOR`),\nprompts for message approval, then commits both repos → advances\nbookmarks → pushes app → finalizes `.claude`. Failures inside the\nlocal mutation window roll both repos back via `jj op restore`;\nafter `push-app` succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and\nrecovery is forward-only.\n\n**Run this flow when you push** — push is discretionary: to\nback up work or to publish interim progress, and always at\nclose-out (see [Versioning](#versioning) on push timing). A\nsingle push can carry one commit or a run of them.\n\n### Run `vc-x1 push`\n\n`<bookmark>` below is the **app-side working branch** — the\nbookmark sitting at the tip of the chain `vc-x1 push` should\nadvance and push. For most cycles this is `main`; for\nfeature-branch work it's the feature-branch name (e.g.\n`init-clone-refactor`). Pass the literal name, not the\nplaceholder. **`.claude` always pushes its `main`** regardless\nof the app-side bookmark passed (see\n[`.claude` cadence](#claude-cadence) — `.claude` is a linear\njournal).\n\n```\nvc-x1 push <bookmark>                                  # interactive (review + $EDITOR)\nvc-x1 push <bookmark> --title \"...\" --body \"...\"       # flags skip $EDITOR\nvc-x1 push <bookmark> --yes --title \"...\" --body \"...\" # full non-interactive\nvc-x1 push <bookmark> --dry-run                        # preview (no side effects)\nvc-x1 push <bookmark> --from commit-app                # resume from specific stage\nvc-x1 push --status                                    # show saved state\nvc-x1 push <bookmark> --restart                        # clear saved state; start fresh\n```\n\n**Bookmark mismatch is currently silent.** `vc-x1 push`\ndoesn't verify that `<bookmark>` matches the working-copy\nchain — passing a bookmark whose tip isn't an ancestor of `@`\nwill (silently) push that bookmark anyway, leaving your work\nunpushed. Always confirm `jj log -r <bookmark>..@` shows the\ncommits you mean to send. A safety check is on the 0.42.0\ntodo (`vc-x1 push: --scope` flag item).\n\nThe two approval gates are surfaced by push itself:\n\n1. **Review** — push prints `jj diff --stat` for both repos and\n   prompts `[y/N]`. Approve = \"the work is done right\".\n2. **Message** — push either uses `--title`/`--body` (non-editor\n   path) or opens `$EDITOR` on a template. Approve = \"the message\n   reads right\".\n\nBoth titles and bodies are the **same** across the two commits;\nonly the `ochid:` trailer differs per repo. Push collects the\npre-commit chids internally so you don't hand-manage them.\n\nFor the full flag list and stage machine, see `vc-x1 push --help`\nand `notes/chores/chores-05.md > Add push subcommand (0.37.0)`.\n\n### Bot communication during the flow\n\nWhen applying the flow on the user's behalf, use plain prose at\neach gate — no insider jargon (\"Gate N signal\", \"Checkpoint N\",\netc.):\n\n1. **After completing the work** — summarize what changed\n   (file-by-file or feature-by-feature, terse) and end with\n   something like:\n\n   > Work complete. Please review. On approval I'll prep the\n   > commit title and body for a second approval before pushing.\n\n2. **After review approval** — present the proposed `$TITLE` and\n   `$BODY` explicitly, then ask permission to run `vc-x1 push`.\n   Do **not** spell out the full\n   `vc-x1 push <bookmark> --yes --title \"$TITLE\" --body \"$BODY\"`\n   invocation by default — it's mechanical and obvious from the\n   title/body. Show it only on explicit request (verbose mode /\n   debugging).\n\n3. **After execution approval** — run the push command. `push`\n   handles commit + bookmark + push + finalize internally;\n   nothing should be output after the push command (finalize is\n   detached and absolute-last; see \"After finalize: stop and\n   wait\" below).\n\n### Pre-step: `vc-x1 sync` (still useful)\n\nPush's preflight runs `vc-x1 sync --check` as its first step. Check\nmode verifies divergence without applying — if anything is `behind`\nor `diverged`, preflight errors and you resolve explicitly with\n`vc-x1 sync --no-check` before re-running push. This keeps the\ntwo approval gates (review, message) authoritative; push never\nperforms an unsupervised rebase.\n\nRunning sync manually before you *start* editing is still cheap\n(one line when clean) and surfaces remote changes earlier:\n\n```\nvc-x1 sync --check                    # dual-repo workspace, verify\nvc-x1 sync --no-check                 # apply (rebase / fast-forward)\nvc-x1 sync --check -R .               # single-repo project\nvc-x1 sync --check --quiet            # silent; exit code signals result\n```\n\nBots and scripts must pass `--check` or `--no-check` explicitly —\ndefaults can shift, explicit flags lock in the contract. Interactive\nuse can take the default (which is `--check`).\n\nOutput shape:\n\n- **Clean**: one line — `sync: N repos, all bookmarks up-to-date`.\n  Note: scope is bookmark-vs-remote tracking, not working-copy\n  cleanliness — `@` may have uncommitted changes; sync doesn't\n  speak to that. Proceed.\n- **Action needed** (`behind` / `diverged`) under `--check`:\n  per-repo fetch + state lines, then a fatal\n  `sync: N repos need action — resolve with vc-x1 sync --no-check\n  and re-run`. Inspect, run `vc-x1 sync --no-check`, then proceed.\n- **`--quiet`**: no output; exit code is the only signal.\n\n### After finalize: stop and wait\n\nAfter `vc-x1 finalize` is launched — **whether mid-session per-step\nor at session end** — you **MUST NEVER** proceed to a next step, edit\nfiles, run tools, or emit any text (prose, recaps, acknowledgements),\nuntil the user explicitly directs you to continue. Treat finalize as\na hard stop for the whole turn. Any final words (e.g. \"next is ...\")\nmust be said in the approval prompt *before* executing finalize; the\nfinalize `Bash` call is the last thing in the turn and nothing\nfollows it.\n\nThis holds even when the next step seems obvious (e.g. \"next is\nN+1\" or \"now I should bump the version and commit the release\").\nWait. The user controls cadence — every push+finalize is a checkpoint\nthey may want to inspect, think about, hand off, or take a break at.\nAuto-proceeding bypasses that checkpoint and produces unwanted writes\nbetween finalize and the next explicit instruction.\n\nExceptions to this rule may emerge later but are not authorized at\nthis stage. Until told otherwise, treat as absolute.\n\n### Late changes after push\n\nIf the app repo needs a tweak after `push-app` succeeded (e.g.\nupdating CLAUDE.md or memory), the commit is immutable. Use\n`--ignore-immutable` to squash the changes into `@-` — the\nbookmark moves with the rewritten commit — then re-push:\n\n```\njj squash --ignore-immutable -R .\njj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R .\n```\n\nIf you squash somewhere other than `@-`, add\n`jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r <target> -R .` between those\ntwo commands so the bookmark lands on the rewritten commit.\n\n`.claude` is also mutable via this pattern when needed, though\npush's `finalize-claude` stage normally handles trailing session\nwrites so you rarely hit this case there.\n\n### Manual finalize fallback\n\nIf push exited before `finalize-claude` (e.g. `--no-finalize`\nwas set, or a failure between `push-app` and `finalize-claude`),\nrun finalize by hand:\n\n```\nvc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log\n```\n\n**Nothing should happen after finalize** — no memory writes, no\ntool calls, no additional output. If any work is done after\nfinalize, run finalize again so the trailing writes are captured.\nDo **not** echo or restate the finalize output — the Bash tool\nalready displays it.\n\n**Clear push's saved state after any out-of-band recovery** —\nmanual `vc-x1 finalize`, manual `jj squash --ignore-immutable`\n+ force-push, etc., all leave `.vc-x1/push-state.toml` pointing\nat a now-stale halt point. Either `rm .vc-x1/push-state.toml`\nor run `vc-x1 push <bookmark> --restart` (which clears and\nrestarts in one go) before the next `vc-x1 push`. Otherwise\npush resumes from a bogus stage and can falsely declare\nsuccess.",
  "new_string": ""
}

To launch your editor as the diff tool:\n\n```\n# ~/.config/jj/config.toml\n[ui]\ndiff-editor = [\"zed\", \"--diff\", \"$left\", \"$right\"]\n```\n\nThen `jj diff --from X --to Y` opens it. Don't `jj edit\n-r @-` to view a past commit — that marks it mutable and\nshifts the working-copy pointer; use `jj diff -r @-` or\n`jj show -r @-`.\n\n[`notes/substep-protocol.md`](notes/substep-protocol.md) is\na longer-form companion (close-out squash recipe, recovery)\nthat predates this rewrite and still uses the old \"sub-step\"\nvocabulary — reconciling it is a follow-up. Correct. The new `### Close-out` and `### Pushing` both treat squash and push as actions taken *after* the close-out commit lands:

1. Close-out **work**: clear `## In Progress`, write `## Done` entry, finalize chores-11 (re-title to final commit title, add As-built ladder + Outcome).
2. **Gate 1** (work review in Zed).
3. **Draft** the close-out commit title + body (this becomes the final `0.56.0` title if we squash).
4. **Gate 2** (message review).
5. **Commit** as bare `0.56.0`.
6. **Then decide** squash-or-keep, looking at the actual ladder.
7. **Push** — either `vc-x1 push main` with a `--squash` workflow, or `vc-x1 push main` as-is for the 4-commit ladder.

So all the deciding-and-deciding happens with the close-out commit already in hand. If we squash, we squash the 3 sub-step commits *into* the bare `0.56.0` (which already has the final title we want). If we keep, we just push the chain.

```
443	### Pushing
444	
445	**Push is discretionary** — done when you want to back up
446	work or publish interim progress, and **always at
447	close-out** (the cycle's result must be published). Interim
448	pushes are a judgment call; nothing forces push-per-commit.
449	
450	The flow is wrapped by `vc-x1 push <bookmark>`, which runs:
451	
452	1. Preflight (`vc-x1 sync --check` + `cargo fmt` / `clippy`
453	   / `test`).
454	2. **Review-gate prompt** — shows `jj diff --stat` for both
455	   repos and asks `[y/N]`.
456	3. **Message-gate prompt** — uses `--title` / `--body` or
457	   opens `$EDITOR`.
458	4. Commits both repos with ochid trailers, advances the
459	   bookmark, `jj git push --bookmark <bookmark>` for the
460	   app, finalizes `.claude` (its single per-push commit).
461	
462	`<bookmark>` is the **app-side working branch** (often
463	`main`, sometimes a feature branch). `.claude` always pushes
464	its `main` regardless of the app-side bookmark. See
465	`vc-x1 push --help` for the full flag list, resumability
466	(`--from`, `--restart`, `--status`), and recovery; CLAUDE.md
467	keeps only the rules that govern bot behavior. `vc-x1 sync
468	--check` may also be run manually before editing to surface
469	remote changes early.
470	
471	**`.claude` cadence — once per push.** The `.claude`
472	working-copy node accumulates session data across the
473	cycle's commits and is described + committed when a push
474	happens — one push, one `.claude` commit, paired with every
475	code commit in that push. Its change ID is stable from the
476	first code commit that references it through to that
477	`.claude` commit (working-copy snapshots, `jj describe`, and
478	the finalize `jj commit` all preserve the change ID), so the
479	`ochid:` trailers resolve across the push.
480	
481	`.claude` is a linear journal — all session work lives on
482	`main`. The repo has no need for parallel feature-branch
483	bookmarks to mirror app-side branches; when the app sits on
484	a feature branch, `.claude` still commits to `main`.
485	Cross-references between sides are carried by the `ochid:`
486	trailer + commit timestamps; that is enough. **Do not
487	create or maintain `.claude` bookmarks that mirror app-side
488	branches** — tried during 0.41.1-6.7, went unused, and
489	risks the bot steering session pushes to the wrong remote
490	ref.
491	
492	**Bot communication at the gates.** Use plain prose — no
493	insider jargon ("Gate N signal", "Checkpoint N", etc.):
494	
495	- **At work-review** — summarize what changed and stop.
496	  "Work complete. Please review."
497	- **At message-review** — present `$TITLE` and `$BODY`
498	  explicitly, then ask permission to commit (or push).
499	  Don't spell out the full
500	  `vc-x1 push <bookmark> --yes --title "$TITLE" --body
501	  "$BODY"` invocation by default; it's mechanical and
502	  obvious from title/body. Show it only on explicit
503	  request.
504	
505	**After finalize: stop and wait.** After `vc-x1 finalize`
506	launches — whether mid-session per-push or at session-end —
507	you **MUST NEVER** proceed to a next step, edit files, run
508	tools, or emit any text (prose, recaps, acknowledgements),
509	until the user explicitly directs you to continue. Treat
510	finalize as a hard stop for the whole turn. Any final words
511	must be said in the approval prompt *before* executing
512	finalize; the finalize `Bash` call is the last thing in the
513	turn and nothing follows it.
514	
515	This holds even when the next step seems obvious. Wait. The
516	user controls cadence — every push+finalize is a checkpoint
517	they may want to inspect, think about, hand off, or take a
518	break at. Auto-proceeding bypasses that checkpoint.
519	
520	If push exits before `finalize-claude` (e.g. failure between
521	`push-app` and `finalize-claude`), run finalize by hand:
522	
523	```
524	vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
525	```
526	
527	Clear push's saved state after any out-of-band recovery —
528	`rm .vc-x1/push-state.toml` or `vc-x1 push <bookmark>
529	--restart` — otherwise push resumes from a stale stage.
530	
531	A late code-repo tweak after `push-app` succeeded (e.g.
532	updating CLAUDE.md or memory) requires `jj squash
```

415	### Close-out
416	
417	The cycle's last commit (bare `X.Y.Z`):
418	
419	- **Move the picked-up item** from `## In Progress` to a
420	  one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) in `## Done`.
421	- **Finalize the chores section.** Sync the section header
422	  to the **final** commit title and update every markdown
423	  anchor back-reference to it; add an `### As-built ladder`
424	  listing the cycle's commits; `### Outcome` notes if
425	  useful.
426	- **Decide squash or keep separate.** Two valid shapes:
427	  - **Squash to one commit** — single entry on the target;
428	    per-commit granularity preserved only in the commit
429	    body's edit list. Right when the work is one logical
430	    change with intermediate validation points. Cost: the
431	    per-commit chores sections collapse into one (its
432	    header becomes the close-out commit title), and every
433	    anchor back-ref to a now-gone section gets re-pointed
434	    — real work, done as part of the squash.
435	  - **Keep separate** — one commit per cycle entry — when
436	    the decomposition is itself informative (distinct
437	    conceptual stages worth showing in `git log`). Each
438	    chores section keeps its own header / `Commits:` ref;
439	    no consolidation churn.
440	- **Update `notes/README.md`** if functionality changed
441	  (new flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).
442	
443	### Pushing
444	
445	**Push is discretionary** — done when you want to back up
446	work or publish interim progress, and **always at
447	close-out** (the cycle's result must be published). Interim
448	pushes are a judgment call; nothing forces push-per-commit.
449

> DEVELOPER

One more thought. Rather than just two choices after close-out, keep multi-step
as-is push all or squash to single then push one. I'm thinking I'd like a third choice.
Create a "trapezoid". That is taking our 3+ commit "branch" and "squashing" it
into a single commit at 0.56.0 so we have:

* 0.56.0
|\
| \
|  * 0.56.0-2
|  |
|  * 0.56.0-1
|  |
|  * 0.56.0-0
| /
|/
* 0.55.0

Maybe that's just a "merge" but I don't think so as I want a linear history
to be 0.55.0 -> 0.56.0 with the option of seeing the actual steps, if desired.

Thoughts?

> AGENT

Yes — technically it's a **non-fast-forward merge**. Your instinct ("I don't think so") is understandable, but in DAG terms `0.56.0` having two parents (0.55.0 and 0.56.0-2) is exactly that.

What makes it feel different from "just a merge" is the **workflow intent**: you want main to *read* linearly while keeping the sub-step history accessible. That's exactly what `git log --first-parent main` (or `jj log --ancestors @ --first-parent` equivalent) gives — follow only the left edge, skip the bundle that came in from the right. Lots of projects use this pattern (Linux kernel topic branches, Phabricator-era workflows). So calling it a non-FF merge is right; treating it as a distinct third close-out shape is also right.

## The three shapes side-by-side

| | main commits | --first-parent view | sub-steps preserved | chores |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Squash** | 1 (`0.56.0`) | 1 | no | collapse |
| **Trapezoid** (non-FF merge) | 4 reachable, **1 on --first-parent** | 1 | yes | per-cycle section keeps its own subs |
| **Keep separate** | 4 | 4 | yes | per-cycle section keeps its own subs |

**Trapezoid pros:** linear main view + preserved sub-step granularity; no commit-message rewrite; chores don't have to consolidate; survives `git show` / `git bisect --first-parent`.

**Trapezoid cons:** default `git log` (no `--first-parent`) shows all commits — fine, sometimes more than wanted; setup is slightly heavier (`jj new <main-tip> <feature-tip>` for the merge commit); the merge commit's `.claude` companion carries a **multi-line ochid** (4 entries: itself + the 3 sub-steps), which is exactly the multi-line-ochid case `notes/forks-multi-user.md` already designs.

## Naming

"Trapezoid" is evocative but unfamiliar — I'd reach for something a git/jj reader recognizes. Options:
- **Merge** (with a `--first-parent` note) — accurate, conventional.
- **Wrap with a merge commit** — explanatory.
- **Non-FF close-out** — git-jargon-y but precise.

I'd use **"Merge (non-FF)"** as the heading and reference `--first-parent` for the linear view.

## Where to codify

Add it as a third option in `### Pushing`'s squash-or-keep description (rename to "Squash, merge, or keep?"). The close-out commit content doesn't change — the shape decision still happens at push time, and the trapezoid is a third push-time shape alongside squash and keep-separate.

This is a Gate-1 iteration on the close-out commit: should I expand `### Pushing` to cover the trapezoid as a third option now (folds into the same close-out commit), or punt as a follow-up cycle? I'd say **now** — it's small, the user is asking for it, and the squash-or-keep paragraph is already being moved in this very commit.

Actually should this be 0.56.0-3 ?

> AGENT

Real question — there are two interpretations of where close-out content lives:

**A. Current convention — close-out IS the bare `0.56.0`.** The merge commit serves *dual* role: it's both the close-out (carries the close-out content: cleared `## In Progress`, finalized chores, etc.) *and* the merge. 4 commits total: `-0`, `-1`, `-2`, `0.56.0` (merge-with-close-out-content).

**B. Close-out is `-3`; bare `0.56.0` is just the merge.** Close-out treated as another work commit (last sub-step). The merge commit on main has no new content — purely structural. 5 commits total: `-0`, `-1`, `-2`, `-3` (close-out content), `0.56.0` (empty merge).

Trade-offs:

| | A | B |
|---|---|---|
| Commit count | 4 | 5 |
| What `bare` means | "close-out lives here" — consistent across squash/merge/keep | "shipped form on main" — but undefined for keep-separate (no bare commit there) |
| Semantic clarity | merge commit conflates content + structure | clean separation |
| Standard practice | non-FF merge often carries merge-time content (kernel-style, GitHub default) | separate-empty-merge less common |

I lean **A**. The "merge commit carries close-out content" pattern is well-established. It also stays consistent across the three shapes — bare always means "close-out is here." Going with B forces an answer for keep-separate too (where there'd be no bare commit), and we'd need to update `### Numbering` in CLAUDE.md.

But you're the one experiencing it. Want to try B for the dogfooding "feel" — see if the extra commit reads better, or buys clarity worth the cost? If yes, this iteration is bigger (rename `@`'s pending version to `-3`, update Cargo.toml/chores/todo/CLAUDE.md `### Numbering` accordingly).

Your call.

```

- `-N` commits carry work (and may carry close-out bookkeeping alongside).
- Bare `X.Y.Z` is the *cycle's landing* on main — squash result, or trapezoid merge — and (in trapezoid) carries only the Cargo bare bump. Minimal.

## docs: refine cycle protocol (0.56.0)

Refines CLAUDE.md's cycle protocol end-to-end. Picked up
from `## Todo` item 1 (codify the move-into-`## In
Progress` workflow); grew through the cycle into a broader
rework:

- **Pickup rule** — on picking up a `## Todo` item, *move*
  its text into `## In Progress` so the section is
  self-describing; the chores opener **mirrors** that
  content so the cycle stays readable once `## In Progress`
  is cleared at close-out; the `## Done` one-liner with a
  chores `[N]` ref is added at close-out.
- **One protocol** — single-step / multi-step / sub-step /
  sub-sub retired; one cycle protocol with three phases:
  **Preparation / Work-N / Close-out**. The `.`-separator
  nested numbering, the trailing-`0`-marks-Preparation
  rule, and the ordering caveat all live in
  `### Numbering`.
- **Push and squash are discretionary** — actions taken
  when wanted (back up, publish, tidy), not modes chosen
  at cycle start; the close-out push is mandatory; the
  squash-vs-keep decision lives at push time.
- **`.claude` once per push** — the bot repo accumulates
  session data across the cycle's commits and commits once
  per push. The per-sub-step `.claude` cadence is gone;
  with push decoupled from the commit cadence, `.claude`
  simply follows the push, and an N:1 push (no squash)
  emits a multi-ochid list in the `.claude` commit.
- **Two-gate review model** — review happens **before**
  each commit: **Gate 1** (work — review the working-copy
  changes in your editor), then write the description,
  then **Gate 2** (message), then commit. ESC-ESC and
  "stop on deviation/question" override. The earlier
  commit-first model (review the committed revision) is
  gone.
- **CLAUDE.md consolidated** — the scattered cycle /
  commit / push material collapses into one linear
  `## Cycle Protocol` that reads top-to-bottom as the flow
  (intro → `### Numbering` → `### Preparation` → `###
  Per-commit flow` → `### Reviewing changes` → `###
  Close-out` → `### Pushing` → `### ochid trailers`).
  `## Committing`, `## Commit Message Style`, `### User
  approval`, `### Versioning`, `### Pre-commit checklist`,
  `## ochid Trailers`, and `## Commit-Push-Finalize Flow`
  are gone; `vc-x1 push` flag/stage/recovery detail
  deferred to `vc-x1 push --help`. CLAUDE.md shrinks ~39%
  (1035 → 632 lines).

### As-built ladder

- 0.56.0-0 Preparation — version bump; populate
  `## In Progress` (pickup of `## Todo` item 1); open
  chores section.
- 0.56.0-1 Work — rewrite `### Versioning` + `## Sub-step
  Workflow` → `## Cycle Protocol`; rewrite the review
  sections (provisionally to commit-first; corrected at
  `-2`); add `notes/forks-multi-user.md` "Staged fork
  integration" subsection.
- 0.56.0-2 Work — the big-lever: collapse 8 sections into
  one linear `## Cycle Protocol`; the two-gate
  review-before-commit model lands here.
- 0.56.0 Close-out — clear `## In Progress`, `## Done`
  one-liner, finalize this chores section, move
  squash-vs-keep text from `### Close-out` to
  `### Pushing`; `Cargo.toml` → `0.56.0`.

### Outcome

A bigger cycle than the original pickup framed. Mid-cycle
re-scoping (`-2` rewriting `-1`'s sections from scratch)
and a review-model correction (two gates, before commit,
instead of commit-first) drove the consolidation. Final
CLAUDE.md reads as one linear protocol section instead of
an eight-section assembly job.

# References

915	  sub-sub retired; one cycle protocol with three phases:

917	  nested numbering, the trailing-`0`-marks-Preparation
918	  rule, and the ordering caveat all live in
919	  `### Numbering`.
920	- **Push and squash are discretionary** — actions taken
921	  when wanted (back up, publish, tidy), not modes chosen
922	  at cycle start; the close-out push is mandatory; the
923	  squash-vs-keep decision lives at push time.
924	- **`.claude` once per push** — the bot repo accumulates
925	  session data across the cycle's commits and commits once
926	  per push. The per-sub-step `.claude` cadence is gone;
927	  with push decoupled from the commit cadence, `.claude`
928	  simply follows the push, and an N:1 push (no squash)
929	  emits a multi-ochid list in the `.claude` commit.
930	- **Two-gate review model** — review happens **before**
931	  each commit: **Gate 1** (work — review the working-copy
932	  changes in your editor), then write the description,
933	  then **Gate 2** (message), then commit. ESC-ESC and
934	  "stop on deviation/question" override. The earlier
935	  commit-first model (review the committed revision) is
936	  gone.

> AGENT

What gitk shows is normal — the trapezoid is real in jj, just not yet visible to git because:

1. **`@` (zpunvows) is the jj working copy** — a mutable commit. In a colocated jj+git repo, git treats it as "the index" (uncommitted) rather than as a real commit. That's the green dot in your gitk.
2. **The "Local uncommitted changes" red dot** is the unsnapshotted working tree (the Cargo.toml `-3` → `0.56.0` edit jj hasn't snapshotted into `@` yet — jj auto-snapshots on the next jj command).
3. **The cycle commits `-0` through `-3` exist as real git objects**, but no git ref points at them. main is still at `0.55.0`. Without a reachable ref, gitk's default view doesn't show them.

To make git see the trapezoid, we need to **finalize zpunvows + advance `main`**:
- `jj commit -m "..." -m "..." -R .` — finalize zpunvows as a real (immutable) git commit; a new empty `@` is created on top.
- `jj bookmark set main -r <zpunvows-cid> -R .` — advance main. In colocated mode, jj exports the bookmark to git's `main` branch, so `main` now points at the merge commit.

After that, `git log main` (and gitk) sees:
- `main` = zpunvows (merge, **two parents**: tspxopvl and lzlkqunp).
- `git log --first-parent main` reads 0.55.0 → 0.56.0 (one jump).
- `git log main` (default) shows all 5 commits — the trapezoid.

So the trapezoid is real; it just hasn't crossed the "publish to git refs" line yet. That happens at Gate 2 + commit + bookmark advance, then push.

Ready for Gate 2 (the merge commit's title + body)?

> DEVELOPER

LG, but couldn't we have executed `vc-x1 push --title "blah title"  --body "blah body" --delay 10 ...` and then gotten both in one commit and there would be no uncommited changes on the bot repo?

> AGENT

Good question — partial yes, but two reasons we went manual:

1. **The trapezoid needs the merge built first.** `vc-x1 push` does commit+bookmark+push+finalize, but its commit step uses plain `jj commit -m`. It doesn't know how to create a two-parent merge commit. The `jj rebase -r @ -d main -d -3` step (and the Cargo bare bump for the merge's content) has to happen before `vc-x1 push` runs — and the new `### Pushing` text I just wrote actually documents that: "Set up with `jj rebase -r <tip> -d <prev> -d <sub-tip>` before invoking `jj git push`."

2. **`vc-x1 push` uses the same title/body for both repos** (single ochid each direction). The trapezoid's `.claude` companion wants a **multi-line ochid** (5 entries — one per app commit in the push) per the multi-ochid case `forks-multi-user.md` designs. `vc-x1 push` doesn't generate that today.

So the actual sequence we could have done:
- Manual: `jj rebase` to build the merge, bump Cargo.toml, cargo cycle.
- Then `vc-x1 push main --title "..." --body "..." --delay 10 ...` — would've finalized the app merge commit + handled `.claude` finalize with the delay (capturing trailing writes).
- But: the `.claude` body would have been a copy of the app title/body with a single ochid → app merge, missing the multi-line ochid to the sub-steps. The trapezoid commits would still land on the remote correctly; only the `.claude` ↔ app pairing fidelity would suffer (loses the per-sub-step pointers).

The all-manual path got the multi-line ochid right. The trade-off was synchronous push (no `--delay 10`), so this very session response goes into the *next* `.claude` commit (the new empty `@` `kuktqzrs`) rather than the just-pushed one. Minor — `vc-x1 finalize --delay 10 --detach` would've captured a few seconds more of trailing writes.

**Follow-up worth queueing**: extend `vc-x1 push` to support (a) trapezoid setup (`--merge` flag that does the `jj rebase` automatically) and (b) multi-line ochid generation for `.claude` when N>1 code commits in the push. Both would let trapezoid runs collapse back to a single `vc-x1 push` invocation.

> DEVELOPER

Right vc-x1 handles the ochid for body magically and we haven't taught it how to handle multiple commits it needs to point to!

> AGENT

Exactly. Today `vc-x1 push` magic for ochid is:
1. Capture `.claude` `@` chid → put as the app commit's `ochid: /.claude/<chid>`.
2. Capture app `@` chid → put as the `.claude` commit's `ochid: /<chid>`.

Both sides 1:1. That works for the squash close-out shape (one code commit ↔ one `.claude` commit) and for plain single-step. It silently does the wrong thing for trapezoid and keep-separate — pointing `.claude` only at the *latest* app commit, dropping the rest.

Teaching it for multi-ochid would be:
- Detect "how many app commits are in this push" (the set `bookmark@origin..bookmark`).
- Emit one `ochid:` line per code commit in `.claude`'s body.
- Code commits keep one ochid each (still 1:1 from their direction — `.claude` is one node per push, so all code commits point at the same `.claude` chid).
- Trapezoid additionally needs the `jj rebase -r <tip> -d <prev> -d <sub-tip>` setup before the commit stage — either a `--shape squash|merge|keep` flag or auto-detect from the working-copy chain.

Worth a `## Todo` entry next cycle when one is opened — it's the natural pickup that unblocks the trapezoid from being a manual flow. Forks-multi-user.md already specs the multi-line ochid format, so this is "wire push to emit it" rather than design work.
